        Idea:
Implement a creative-work mode in megaplan, generalizing today's `--mode joke` into a `--mode creative --form <form>` system with a real provocation engine.

## The provocation engine

The brief fires a small set of provocations at the executor — adversarial prompts that demand an act on the work, not introspection. Three vectors:

- **Cuts** — strip / expose / kill. Example: "Cut the line you'd fight hardest to keep. If the piece is worse, you were right; if it's the same, you killed a darling."
- **Forces** — perform a transformation on the piece. Example: "Halve it. Write the line that survives."
- **Sparks** — the brief itself supplies foreign material the maker reacts to. This is the new vector vs joke mode today. Sub-types:
  - Hostile gifts (an image, line, or detail invented in the brief and asserted to belong)
  - Substitutions ("what if the protagonist were the antagonist of this same scene")
  - Inversions ("the version of this that's a confession, not a performance")
  - Forced transplants (steal a structure from a different form: tarot spread, recipe, courtroom transcript)
  - Borrowed moves (steal one specific move from a master in this form, named)
  - Hostile claims ("this piece is afraid of the thing it's actually about" — refute or absorb)
  - Generative constraints (half the words forbidden, no character speaks, must rhyme)
  - Sensory escalation (what does it smell like / sound like in the next room)
  - Dares (name the move that would get you booed; try it)

Default firing pattern: **one cut, one force, one spark per pass.** Three is plenty; five is a lecture. The prep phase chooses the specific provocations targeted at the draft state — over-explained drafts get cuts on the unsaid; safe drafts get risk/dare sparks; bloated drafts get force-halve; replaceable-feeling drafts get borrowed-move + "who could have made this."

## The stance

Every executor task output includes a stance field: in the maker's authentic voice, naming the provocation engaged, the angle taken, and what changed. Hard constraints to prevent LLM-flavor sand:

- ≤50 words
- First person
- Names the specific provocation engaged
- No hedging verbs (considered / tried / attempted / sought to)
- Must take a position someone could disagree with

A sub-provocation fires at the stance itself: "Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger."

Stance lives in per-task receipts and is bubbled into a "director's notes" sidecar artifact. NOT inline in the final piece — the artifact must stand alone.

## Robustness mapping

Creative work has a different process curve than code: it peaks and then declines into over-sanded mush. Robustness should mean *different angles, harder targets, more diverse provocateur voices* — not just more cycles.

- **Light** — single pass, one cut + one spark (skip the force), stance ≤2 sentences, no critique cycle. For sketches and throwaways. Failure mode protected against: over-process killing a quick instinct.
- **Standard** — one critique/revise cycle. Full triad on prep. Critique fires *different* provocations from a different angle (no relitigating addressed material). Revise stance must explicitly contrast the prior stance.
- **Robust** — multiple cycles with *different provocateur voices*: formalist (structure/economy), iconoclast (risk/lie/dare), audience (stakes/specificity for one named person). Review evaluates full scene canvas + stance authenticity + alignment.

## Executor stop-affordance

Let the maker signal "the next pass would damage the piece" with a stance defending why. Code mode shouldn't have this (more review = safer); creative must. Without it, robust mode reliably over-edits.

## Generalize joke → creative-work

- New mode: `--mode creative --form <form>` with joke as the first registered form
- Existing `--mode joke` continues to work as an alias for `--mode creative --form joke` — no breakage of existing plans
- Prove the abstraction by adding a second form: prep phase picks the cheapest validation form (poem or short ad copy)
- Form-specific prompt fragments inject into shared scaffolds; we do NOT fork five new prompt files per form

## Design branches the prep phase should resolve

The following are real branches with arguments either way — settle them in prep, not in code:

1. Stance field shape: single string vs structured (`challenge_engaged` / `angle_taken` / `what_changed`)
2. Director's notes destination: separate sidecar file, YAML frontmatter on the artifact, or only in receipts
3. Stop-affordance signal: new outcome status, stance flag, or separate halt-task type
4. Provocateur voices in robust mode: fixed roster (formalist / iconoclast / audience) vs model-chosen per cycle
5. Robustness → provocation-count mapping: formal table vs executor discretion within bounds
6. Whether to retire `--primary-criterion` as joke-only and lift it to creative-work-wide, or keep both surfaces

## Out of scope

- New forms beyond the second validation form
- Non-text creative outputs (audio/visual)
- Team-of-makers workflows
- Backwards-compat for any pre-v0.21 joke-mode plan files (current plans untouched; new plans use new schema)

## Primary criterion (for prep guidance)

Sharpest provocation system that survives over-process. The system should produce work that has *a stance*, took risks, made specific choices, and refused something — and should produce that work without grinding it into nervous mush via too many cycles. If the system is "more rigorous" but the output is more sanded, we have failed. The stance authenticity check is the single most load-bearing piece — it is what converts the system from "external pressure" into "training the maker as their own provocateur."

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Creative-Work Mode + Provocation Engine

## Overview

Generalize today's `--mode joke` into `--mode creative --form <form>` with a registered-form system, replace the static joke "lens" critique with a real **provocation engine** (cuts / forces / sparks), add a per-task **stance** field with hard constraints, route stances into a **director's notes sidecar**, add an executor **stop-affordance**, and remap robustness so higher levels mean *different angles and provocateur voices*, not more cycles.

Current shape:
- Mode selection at `megaplan/cli.py:885`; init validation at `megaplan/handlers/init.py:48-72` (writes `state.config.mode`, `output_path`, `primary_criterion`).
- Prompt dispatch by `state.config.mode` at `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py:118-170` (string-equality on `"joke"`).
- Joke prompts: `prep_joke.py`, `critique_joke.py`, `revise_joke.py`, `execute_joke.py`, `review_joke.py`.
- Static joke critique lenses: `megaplan/audits/robustness.py:140-217` (`JOKE_CRITIQUE_CHECKS`, `joke_checks_for_robustness`).
- Schemas: `megaplan/schemas.py` (`finalize.json`, `execution.json`); finalize task `status` enum is `pending|done|skipped|blocked`; execution `task_updates.status` is `done|skipped|blocked`.
- Tests: `tests/test_handle_init_joke_mode.py`, `tests/test_joke_mode_smoke.py`, plus `tests/bakeoff/*` (currently modified on this branch — leave those alone unless impacted).

The whole change is contained inside `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/init.py`, `megaplan/prompts/`, `megaplan/audits/robustness.py`, `megaplan/schemas.py`, plus a new `megaplan/forms/` package and tests under `tests/`.

Form-specific content (lenses, prep checklists, beat IDs) lives **inside the forms registry**; prompt builders read from a single `Form` object so we don't fork five new prompt files per form. The shared scaffolds in `prep_joke.py`/`critique_joke.py`/etc. become `prep_creative.py`/etc., parameterized by the active `Form`. The existing files stay as thin shims (or are renamed and re-exported) so the prompt-dispatch import surface keeps working.

Joke-mode plans created before this lands keep working unchanged: `state.config.mode == "joke"` continues to dispatch through the same code paths. New plans set `mode = "creative"` and `form = "joke"|"poem"`. The CLI accepts both forms (joke is an alias).

---

## Phase 1: Foundation — forms registry, mode plumbing, schemas

### Step 1: Create the forms registry (`megaplan/forms/__init__.py`, `megaplan/forms/joke.py`, `megaplan/forms/poem.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Add** `megaplan/forms/__init__.py` exporting:
   - `Form` dataclass with fields: `id: str`, `display_name: str`, `output_extension: str`, `beat_ids: tuple[str, ...]`, `prep_checklist: tuple[str, ...]` (form-specific brief items), `provocations: ProvocationCatalog`, `stance_voice_hint: str`, `provocateur_voices: tuple[ProvocateurVoice, ...]`.
   - `ProvocationCatalog` dataclass with `cuts: tuple[Provocation, ...]`, `forces: tuple[Provocation, ...]`, `sparks: tuple[Provocation, ...]`.
   - `Provocation` dataclass with `id: str`, `vector: Literal["cut","force","spark"]`, `subtype: str` (e.g. `"hostile_gift"`, `"halve"`, `"darling"`), `prompt_text: str`, `targets: tuple[str, ...]` (draft-state tags this provocation is meant for: `"over_explained"`, `"safe"`, `"bloated"`, `"replaceable"`, `"any"`).
   - `ProvocateurVoice` dataclass with `id: str` (e.g. `"formalist"`, `"iconoclast"`, `"audience"`), `persona_text: str`, `vector_bias: tuple[str, ...]`.
   - `FORMS: dict[str, Form]` registry, `register(form)`, `get_form(id)`.

2. **Add** `megaplan/forms/joke.py` populating a `Form(id="joke", ...)` with the existing 9 joke lenses migrated to provocations. Each existing lens becomes a `Provocation` with `vector="spark"` and a `subtype` from the brief's spark sub-types (e.g. `subtext_inversion → spark/inversion`, `genre_swap → spark/forced_transplant`). Add ~3 cuts ("cut the line you'd fight hardest to keep", "kill the explanation", "strip the laugh-track beat"), ~3 forces ("halve it", "no character speaks", "must rhyme by end of beat"). Beat IDs: `("opening","inciting","obstacle","turn","button")`. Provocateur voices: `formalist`, `iconoclast`, `audience` with persona text in this file.

3. **Add** `megaplan/forms/poem.py` — the second validation form (per brief: "cheapest validation form (poem or short ad copy)"). Beat IDs: `("opening_image","turn","close")`. Reuse vector definitions but with poem-specific prompt text (e.g. force "halve it. write the line that survives.", spark "borrow one move from a master poet, named"). Smaller catalog is fine — three of each vector minimum so the firing pattern works.

4. **Register** both forms at `megaplan/forms/__init__.py` import time.

### Step 2: Generalize mode/form CLI surface (`megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/init.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Update** `megaplan/cli.py:885-899` to add `creative` to `--mode` choices and add `--form` argument (choices come from `megaplan.forms.FORMS.keys()`). Help text: `"Creative work form (e.g. joke, poem). Required with --mode creative."`.
2. **Update** `megaplan/handlers/init.py:48-72`:
   - Accept `mode == "joke"` and treat it as `mode = "creative"`, `form = "joke"` (alias). Same for any future single-form aliases.
   - When `mode == "creative"`, require `--form` and validate against `FORMS`; require `--output`; allow `--primary-criterion`.
   - Persist `state["config"]["form"] = form` alongside `mode`.
3. **Resolve design branch #6 (default):** `--primary-criterion` becomes valid for all `--mode creative` (any form), not joke-only. Update the existing `if raw_primary_criterion and mode != "joke"` check at `megaplan/handlers/init.py:54` to allow it for any creative mode.
4. **Backward compat:** plans whose state already has `mode == "joke"` (no `form` field) keep working. The dispatcher (Step 4) treats missing `form` as `"joke"` when `mode == "joke"`.

### Step 3: Extend schemas for stance, sidecar, stop-affordance (`megaplan/schemas.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Extend** `execution.json` `task_updates[]` (line 357) to add optional fields:
   - `stance: { challenge_engaged: string, angle_taken: string, what_changed: string }` (resolves design branch #1 → structured; cheaper to validate the ≤50-word and "no hedging verbs" rule on a single composed string).
   - `stop_signal: { requested: bool, defense: string }` (resolves design branch #3 → stance flag, not new outcome status; keeps the existing `done|skipped|blocked` enum untouched).
2. **Extend** `finalize.json` `tasks[]` (line 264) to add optional `stance` mirroring the same shape (so receipts carry it through to the directors-notes assembly).
3. **Add** a new artifact entry `directors_notes.json` with shape:
   ```json
   {
     "form": "joke",
     "primary_criterion": "...",
     "passes": [
       {
         "iteration": 1,
         "provocations_fired": [{"id": "...", "vector": "cut", "subtype": "darling"}],
         "stances": [{"task_id": "T1", "challenge_engaged": "...", "angle_taken": "...", "what_changed": "..."}],
         "stop_requested": false
       }
     ]
   }
   ```
   This is the "separate sidecar file" answer to design branch #2. Receipts also carry `stance` (already covered by extending `finalize.json`), so we get both.

---

## Phase 2: Provocation engine + stance enforcement

### Step 4: Build provocation selection logic (`megaplan/forms/provocations.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Add** `select_provocations(form: Form, *, robustness: str, iteration: int, draft_state_tags: tuple[str, ...], prior_provocation_ids: set[str]) -> tuple[Provocation, ...]`.
2. **Default firing pattern (resolves design branch #5 → formal table within bounds):**
   - `light`: `(1 cut, 1 spark)` — skip the force per brief.
   - `standard`: `(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark)` on prep, then on critique iteration N>1 fire a *different* triad biased away from `prior_provocation_ids`.
   - `robust` / `superrobust`: `(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark)` per cycle, but each cycle uses a different `ProvocateurVoice` from `form.provocateur_voices`. Voice rotates `formalist → iconoclast → audience → ...`. Resolves design branch #4 → fixed roster (deterministic; auditable; honors brief's named voices).
3. **Targeting:** prefer provocations whose `targets` overlap with `draft_state_tags`. Deterministic tie-break by `id`. If no match, fall back to `targets == ("any",)`.
4. **Add** `select_provocateur_voice(form, iteration) -> ProvocateurVoice | None` (returns None for light/standard).
5. **Add** `_draft_state_tags(state, plan_dir) -> tuple[str, ...]`: heuristics on the latest plan markdown to tag `over_explained` (long prose-to-beat ratio), `safe` (no flagged adjectives like "weird", "risky"), `bloated` (>N words), `replaceable` (no named characters/specifics). Keep this dumb-but-deterministic; the executor still chooses how to engage.

### Step 5: Rewrite critique + revise as the provocation engine (`megaplan/prompts/critique_creative.py`, `megaplan/prompts/revise_creative.py`)
**Scope:** Large

1. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/critique_creative.py`:
   - Replaces the lens-based `_critique_joke_prompt`. Takes `form` from registry.
   - Calls `select_provocations(...)` and `select_provocateur_voice(...)`.
   - Renders a prompt that names: the chosen voice persona, exactly the (1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark) targeted at the current draft state, and instructions to **commit** to one concrete proposal per provocation (no hedging, no menus).
   - Standard rule: critique iteration must fire *different* provocations from the prior pass — pass `prior_provocation_ids` from the previous iteration's `directors_notes.json` entry.
   - The output template follows the existing `findings`/`flags` shape so `flag_registry` still works — no schema rewrite needed.
2. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/revise_creative.py`:
   - Mirrors `revise_joke.py` but reads provocation IDs out of the flags rather than lens IDs. Same canvas shape (premise, tone, characters, etc.) but **driven by `form.beat_ids`** (so poem mode renders `opening_image / turn / close`, not screenwriting beats).
3. **Update** `megaplan/audits/robustness.py`:
   - Keep `joke_checks_for_robustness` exporting (back-compat) but reimplement it to call into the form registry: `joke_checks_for_robustness = lambda r: form_provocations_as_checks(get_form("joke"), r)`.
   - Add `form_provocations_as_checks(form, robustness) -> tuple[CritiqueCheckSpec, ...]` that maps the selected provocations to the existing `CritiqueCheckSpec` shape so downstream validation (`validate_critique_checks`) keeps working unchanged.
4. **Update** `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py:137-153`:
   - Add a `_resolve_form(state)` helper: returns `get_form(state.config.form)` if `mode == "creative"`, else `get_form("joke")` if `mode == "joke"` (alias), else `None`.
   - Replace the `if mode == "joke"` block with `if mode in {"creative", "joke"}` and route through the new `*_creative.py` builders, passing `form` in.
   - Keep `_prep_joke_prompt`, `_critique_joke_prompt`, etc. as thin wrappers that call the new creative versions with `form=get_form("joke")` so the existing imports / `__all__` still resolve.

### Step 6: Stance field in execute prompts + validator (`megaplan/prompts/execute_creative.py`, `megaplan/prompts/execute_joke.py`, new `megaplan/forms/stance.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Add** `megaplan/forms/stance.py`:
   - `STANCE_HEDGING_VERBS = ("considered","tried","attempted","sought to","aimed to","worked to")`.
   - `validate_stance(stance: dict) -> list[str]` returning a list of violations:
     - missing `challenge_engaged` / `angle_taken` / `what_changed`,
     - composed string > 50 words,
     - first-person check: composed string lacks any of `("i ","i'm","i've","my ")`,
     - hedging-verb hit,
     - position check: heuristic — must contain at least one of a configurable set of "claim" markers (e.g. `"because"`, `"refused"`, `"chose"`, `"killed"`) OR be flagged for human review.
   - Pure function, no I/O. The harness enforces it post-hoc and bubbles violations into a stance sub-provocation on the next pass (per brief: "Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too.").

2. **Add** `megaplan/prompts/execute_creative.py`:
   - Generalizes `execute_joke.py`. Reads `form.beat_ids` for `sections_written`. Reads `form.stance_voice_hint` to seed the stance prompt.
   - Adds explicit stance instructions to the requirements block: shape, ≤50 words, first person, names the specific provocation engaged, no hedging verbs, must take a position someone could disagree with.
   - Adds stop-affordance instructions: executor MAY set `stop_signal.requested = true` with a `defense` (1 sentence) explaining why the next pass would damage the piece. Code mode never sets this.
3. **Refactor** `megaplan/prompts/execute_joke.py` to a 5-line shim: `_execute_joke_prompt = partial(_execute_creative_prompt, form=get_form("joke"))`, same for batch. Keep `__all__` so the prompts package re-export still works.
4. **Update** `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py:144-145` to dispatch to `_execute_creative_prompt` for `mode == "creative"`.

### Step 7: Honor stop-affordance + write directors-notes sidecar (`megaplan/_core/io.py` or wherever execution result is consumed; `megaplan/handlers/`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Audit** where `execution.json` is consumed and the next critique/execute cycle is decided. (Likely in the auto driver / orchestrator — search for the place that increments iteration after review and decides whether to run another critique/revise cycle.) Add a check: when `mode == "creative"` AND any `task_updates[].stop_signal.requested == true` AND review verdict is `approved`, halt further critique/revise cycles even if robustness budget remains. Record the halt + defense text in a new `meta.notes` entry and in `directors_notes.json`.
2. **Add** a small assembler in `megaplan/forms/directors_notes.py`:
   - `assemble_directors_notes(plan_dir, state) -> dict`: walks the plan dir for per-iteration critique flags + finalize task stances + execute stop signals, builds the schema from Step 3.
   - Called once at the end of each execute and at finalize; writes `plan_dir/directors_notes.json`.
3. **Add** stance validation enforcement: when the harness ingests `execution.json`, run `validate_stance` on each `task_updates[].stance`. Violations are logged to `meta.notes` and surface as a `flags` entry on the next critique pass tagged `"stance_authenticity"`. Do NOT hard-reject the execution — soft enforcement so we don't lose the work.

## Phase 3: Tests + validation

### Step 8: Unit tests for forms + provocation selection + stance validator (`tests/test_forms_registry.py`, `tests/test_provocations.py`, `tests/test_stance_validator.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `tests/test_forms_registry.py`: assert `get_form("joke")` and `get_form("poem")` exist, both have ≥3 cuts/forces/sparks, joke has 5 beat IDs and poem has 3.
2. **Add** `tests/test_provocations.py`:
   - `light` returns 2 provocations (no force).
   - `standard` returns 3 (1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark).
   - Calling `select_provocations` twice with `prior_provocation_ids` from the first call returns a *disjoint* set on standard.
   - `robust` rotates voices across iterations 1, 2, 3.
   - Targeting: provocation with `targets=("over_explained",)` is preferred when that tag is present.
3. **Add** `tests/test_stance_validator.py`:
   - Valid stance passes.
   - >50 words → violation.
   - Hedging verb ("considered") → violation.
   - Third-person ("the writer chose") → violation.
   - No claim marker → violation.

### Step 9: Mode/form CLI integration tests (`tests/test_handle_init_creative_mode.py`, update `tests/test_handle_init_joke_mode.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `tests/test_handle_init_creative_mode.py`:
   - `--mode creative --form joke --output scenes/x.md` succeeds.
   - `--mode creative` without `--form` raises `invalid_args`.
   - `--mode creative --form unknown` raises `invalid_args`.
   - `--mode creative --form poem --primary-criterion "tightest image"` accepted (resolves branch #6).
2. **Update** `tests/test_handle_init_joke_mode.py`: confirm `--mode joke` still works AND now produces `state.config.form == "joke"`. Update assertion accordingly.

### Step 10: Prompt builder tests + dispatcher tests (`tests/test_prompts_creative.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `tests/test_prompts_creative.py`:
   - `_critique_creative_prompt(form=joke, robustness="standard")` mentions exactly 1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark by name.
   - `_critique_creative_prompt(form=poem, ...)` references poem beats (`opening_image`, etc.) not screenplay beats.
   - `_execute_creative_prompt` includes the stance shape + ≤50-word + no-hedging instructions.
   - Iteration 2 critique excludes provocation IDs that fired in iteration 1 (set up minimal `directors_notes.json` fixture in `plan_dir`).
   - Dispatcher: `create_claude_prompt("critique", state_with_mode_joke, ...)` and `create_claude_prompt("critique", state_with_mode_creative_form_joke, ...)` produce equivalent prompts (back-compat).

### Step 11: End-to-end smoke (`tests/test_creative_mode_smoke.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** smoke test mirroring `tests/test_joke_mode_smoke.py`:
   - Init `--mode creative --form poem --output poems/p.md`, run prep+plan+critique+revise+execute+review with mocked agents that produce minimal valid JSON (including a valid stance).
   - Assert `directors_notes.json` is written with at least one stance entry and one fired provocation.
   - Assert stop-signal halts further cycles when set.

### Step 12: Run focused tests, then full suite
**Scope:** Small

1. **Run** `pytest tests/test_forms_registry.py tests/test_provocations.py tests/test_stance_validator.py -x` first (cheapest validation).
2. **Run** `pytest tests/test_handle_init_creative_mode.py tests/test_handle_init_joke_mode.py tests/test_prompts_creative.py -x` next.
3. **Run** `pytest tests/test_creative_mode_smoke.py tests/test_joke_mode_smoke.py -x` after the wiring is in place.
4. **Run** the full `pytest tests/` to catch any regression in unrelated tests (especially `tests/bakeoff/*`, which is currently dirty on this branch but should be unaffected by mode work).

---

## Execution Order

1. Phase 1 (forms registry, mode/form plumbing, schemas) before any prompt rewrite — every later phase depends on the registry existing.
2. Within Phase 1: Step 1 (forms package) → Step 2 (CLI/init) → Step 3 (schemas), in that order.
3. Phase 2: Step 4 (selection logic) → Step 5 (critique/revise rewrite) → Step 6 (execute + stance) → Step 7 (sidecar + stop-affordance honoring). Steps 5 and 6 each depend on Step 4.
4. Phase 3 tests can begin in parallel with Step 5 once Steps 1-4 are committed (registry + selection are pure-functional and testable on their own).
5. The joke→creative shim in Step 5 is the riskiest moment for back-compat — land it with both `tests/test_joke_mode_smoke.py` and `tests/test_creative_mode_smoke.py` green before moving on.

## Validation Order

1. Pure-function unit tests first (registry, selection, stance validator) — no fixtures, no plan dir.
2. Init/CLI tests next — fast, isolated.
3. Prompt builder tests — fixture-based but no agent calls.
4. End-to-end smoke tests with mocked agents.
5. Full pytest suite to catch regressions in adjacent code (especially the in-flight bakeoff changes on this branch).

    "Second validation form \u2014 I picked 'poem' over 'short ad copy' because it's the most different from joke (different beat IDs, different voice) and stresses the abstraction harder. If you'd rather have ad copy as the second form (closer to joke; commercial register), say so before Phase 1 lands."
  ],
  "success_criteria": [
    {
      "criterion": "`--mode creative --form joke --output scenes/x.md` initializes a plan and writes state.config.form='joke', mode='creative'",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`--mode joke --output scenes/x.md` continues to work and writes state.config.form='joke' (alias preserved)",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`--mode creative --form poem --output poems/p.md` initializes successfully (proves the abstraction with a second form)",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "`--mode creative` without `--form` raises a clear invalid_args error",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Provocation selector returns the brief-specified firing pattern: light=(1 cut, 1 spark), standard=(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark), robust=(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark) per cycle with rotating provocateur voice",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Standard-robustness critique iteration N>1 fires *different* provocations from iteration N-1 (no relitigating)",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Robust-robustness rotates fixed-roster provocateur voices (formalist \u2192 iconoclast \u2192 audience) across cycles",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Stance validator rejects: >50 words, missing first person, hedging verbs (considered/tried/attempted/sought to), missing claim marker",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Execute prompt for creative mode includes stance instructions (shape, \u226450 words, first person, names the provocation, no hedging) and stop-affordance instructions",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Execute prompt for code mode does NOT include stop-affordance instructions (per brief: 'Code mode shouldn't have this')",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "directors_notes.json sidecar is written at plan_dir and contains: form, primary_criterion, per-pass provocations_fired, per-pass stances, stop_requested flag",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "When task_update.stop_signal.requested=true and review verdict is approved, the orchestrator halts further critique/revise cycles and records the halt + defense in meta.notes",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Existing tests in tests/test_handle_init_joke_mode.py and tests/test_joke_mode_smoke.py pass after migration (back-compat)",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Full pytest suite passes with no regressions outside the touched files",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Prompt builders for poem mode reference poem beat IDs (opening_image / turn / close), not screenplay beats",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "No new prompt file is forked per form \u2014 joke-specific and poem-specific prompt content lives inside the form registry data, not in separate prompt files",
      "priority": "should",
      "requires": [
        "read_files"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Prompt files (`*_creative.py`) stay under ~250 lines each \u2014 the heavy content moves to the forms registry",
      "priority": "should",
      "requires": [
        "read_files"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Stance authenticity check actually fires as a sub-provocation when validator finds violations (the brief calls this 'the single most load-bearing piece')",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Director's notes JSON is parseable, well-formed, and matches the documented schema",
      "priority": "must",
      "requires": [
        "run_tests"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Real end-to-end run with `--mode creative --form poem` produces output that reads as a poem with a defensible stance (not generic LLM mush)",
      "priority": "info",
      "requires": [
        "subjective_judgment"
      ]
    },
    {
      "criterion": "Real end-to-end run at `--robustness robust` does NOT produce visibly over-edited / sanded output compared to standard (the brief's primary criterion: 'sharpest provocation system that survives over-process')",
      "priority": "info",
      "requires": [
        "subjective_judgment"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "assumptions": [
    "Joke-mode plans created before this lands keep working: state.config.mode='joke' (no 'form' field) is treated as creative+joke by the dispatcher. Per the brief's 'no backwards-compat for any pre-v0.21 joke-mode plan files' line, this is just convenience for in-flight plans, not a hard requirement.",
    "The `--mode joke` CLI flag remains as an alias for `--mode creative --form joke` (the brief explicitly wants this \u2014 'no breakage of existing plans').",
    "The existing flag_registry / flags array structure is reused for the provocation engine output (each fired provocation = one FLAG-<provocation-id>). This avoids a parallel flag system and lets revise/gate/review keep working unchanged.",
    "Robustness mapping in the brief overrides the existing tiny/light/standard/robust/superrobust enum values: I'm mapping superrobust \u2192 same as robust for creative mode (more cycles is the failure mode the brief warns against). Tiny stays as-is (no critique).",
    "Stance validator is soft-enforced (logged + bubbled into next critique as a sub-provocation) rather than hard-rejecting the execution. Hard rejection would force a re-run for a hedging-verb violation, which would burn cost and contradict the brief's 'don't grind into nervous mush' principle.",
    "directors_notes.json is JSON only (machine-readable, parseable by future tooling). If you want a markdown rendering for human reading, that's an easy follow-up but adds prompt+writer code I'd rather defer.",
    "Form registry lives at megaplan/forms/ as a new package. Forms are coded in Python, not loaded from data files \u2014 the brief said 'form-specific prompt fragments inject into shared scaffolds; we do NOT fork five new prompt files per form,' which I read as 'one Python file per form is fine, the prompts themselves stay shared.'",
    "Poem (not ad copy) is the second form \u2014 it pushes the abstraction harder by needing different beat IDs and a different stance voice hint."
  ],
  "structure_warnings": []

      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i checked the skip caller path against the real hook behavior and `removefromqueue(entryid)` looks redundant. `scoring.skipentry(entryid)` optimistically adds the id to `skippedentryids` in `src/hooks/usescoring.ts:245-249`, and `usecarouselqueue` already reactively filters skipped entries from `submissions[]` in `src/hooks/usecarouselqueue.ts:154-165`. calling `removefromqueue()` immediately afterward is safe, but it duplicates an existing state transition and should be justified as an explicit ux optimization rather than required logic.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the direct ui sign-in caller is still important here. `scorepanel` invokes its own `onrequiresignin` prop when an unauthenticated user interacts with the score ui (`src/components/scorepanel.tsx:499-515`), which is independent of the scoring hook's internal `requireauth()` path. the plan should be read as requiring that caller to dispatch `open_sign_in_modal`; otherwise one caller remains broken even if `submitscore()` is correctly wired.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-034",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: i rechecked the caller paths after the revision. the raw-schema caller path through `validate_payload()` is now accounted for, but the runtime-schema caller path still is not: `strict_schema()` only reaches the on-disk runtime schemas through `ensure_runtime_layout()`, while current tests like `tests/test_schemas.py` and `tests/test_parallel_review.py` consume the already-written repo copy directly. the plan does not yet say how that caller path gets refreshed or asserted after the code change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-051",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the real callers for these paths are shell commands that expect actual files. reigh's readme uploads an idea file into `/workspace/megaplan-idea-2week.txt` before calling `megaplan init`, and `chain.sh` immediately checks `-f \"$spec\"` before running `megaplan chain --spec \"$spec\"`. the revised plan now constructs equivalent callers from `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec`, but still never adds the corresponding file-transfer or materialization step, so those callers would receive nonexistent arguments.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-065",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: batch execute callers still need a clearer identity contract. `handle_execute_one_batch()` can be invoked once per global batch in the same plan iteration, and the revised plan tells every invocation to use `phase=\"execute\"`, `iteration=state[\"iteration\"]`, and `attempt=1`. those actual caller arguments are not enough to distinguish batch receipts unless the plan changes the filename/attempt semantics or emits only one final aggregate receipt for `--batch` runs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint1-step-receipts"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-070",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the planned judge resolver's inputs against `load_profiles()` and `parse_agent_spec()`. the caller will receive profile phase values as strings like `claude`, `codex`, or `hermes:glm-5.1`; to handle all actual values, the resolver should parse each spec into `(agent, model)` and compare canonical agent/model identities rather than doing raw string membership against `['claude', 'codex', 'gpt-5']`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the mobile problem is broader than entrypage. `submissionscarouselpage` already has a pre-existing mobile gap because its score panel is desktop-only and its footer actions are also hidden on mobile (`src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347-354` plus `src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`). the revised plan says entrypage should 'match the carouselpage pattern', which would spread that existing gap into the one page that currently has working mobile controls.",

      "id": "DEBT-049",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: input provisioning is the broader unresolved concept here. the repo's current railway workflow does not just run commands; it also stages runtime artifacts into `/workspace` first, such as the uploaded idea text in readme line 103 and the expected `/workspace/chain.yaml` consumed by `chain.sh`. the revised plan fixes the runner dispatch itself, but it still treats `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec` as if those files will already exist without describing how cloud deploy makes that true.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

    {
      "id": "DEBT-057",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the broader concept is now the validation surface rather than the runtime surface. hermes imports already appear in two non-execution helpers via bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`, so the plan's claim that the helper-plus-gate pattern is self-correcting across future codebase evolution is too broad unless the guard also accounts for bare imports, not just `from ... import ...` forms.",

      "id": "DEBT-060",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: while the docstring on apply_profile_expansion correctly documents the recovery side, the root cause in megaplan/auto.py (_phase_command omitting --profile) has no comment explaining why the flag is intentionally dropped. a future maintainer might add --profile to the subprocess command without realizing it would make the state fallback redundant. however, adding --profile would be harmless because apply_profile_expansion would simply use args.profile directly, so no functional breakage would occur.",

      "id": "DEBT-062",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: execute receipt identity: the plan introduces a new collision for multi-batch `--batch` runs. it says execute emits one receipt per handler invocation and `attempt` stays 1, while step 11 emits non-final and final batch receipts with the same `phase=\"execute\"` and `iteration=state[\"iteration\"]`; the plan-dir filename remains `step_receipt_execute_v<iteration>.json`. running batch 1 and then batch 2 in the same iteration would overwrite the plan-dir receipt and create multiple jsonl rows with the same `(plan_id, phase, iteration, attempt)`, contradicting the brief's one-receipt-per-identity contract.",

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-009",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname=\"hidden md:block\">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-010",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "move-the-entry-page-into-20260401-0450"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-029",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "robust-review-v2"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-032",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-6-remaining-openai-20260409-0502"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-046",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new pre-extraction step that migrates `pinnedshotgroups` out of core is still under-specified for compatibility. current runtime and test code reads `config.pinnedshotgroups` directly across `hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts`, `lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`, `hooks/useshotgroups.ts`, `components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx`, `hooks/usetimelinetrackmanagement.ts`, `lib/serialize.test.ts`, `lib/migrate.test.ts`, and many other tests, so landing that migration before extraction needs a dual-read/projection shim or a clearly synchronized update plan instead of only a schema/serializer note.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "design-plan-to-extract-the-20260419-1637"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-050",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: runner inputs: the revised auto-mode snippet calls `$(cat ${idea_file})` and the chain-mode path runs `mp-chain ${chain_spec}`, but step 7's deploy flow still only does `railway link`, `railway variables --set`, and `railway up`. there is no corresponding step that copies the idea file or chain spec into `/workspace`, so a literal implementation would boot into auto/chain mode and immediately fail on missing input files.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-056",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised 'positive structural check' is still not complete enough to justify the plan text that says future direct hermes imports will fail fast by construction. step 3.4 only greps `from (run_agent|hermes_state) import ...`, and step 9.1 only bans `ast.importfrom` nodes for those modules, but the current repo already uses bare `import run_agent` in `megaplan/workers.py:1590` and `megaplan/_core/io.py:270`; a future regression that copied that import style into another execution path would bypass the stated guard without tripping the plan's validation.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "vendor-hermes-agent-into-the-20260422-0112"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-064",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: batch execute: recurring debt: the revised step 11 still has an implementation-ordering problem for high-drift blocking in `handle_execute_one_batch`. in the real code, `blocking_reasons` is converted to `blocked` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:594-601`, and only later does the final-batch block build and write `execution.json` at `megaplan/execute/core.py:611-621`. the plan says to compute final-batch drift after that aggregate write but also append the high-drift reason before `blocked = bool(blocking_reasons)` is evaluated, which is impossible without explicitly moving/restructuring the existing `blocked` decision and the `state[\"current_state\"] = state_executed` assignment.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

        ]

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new judge auto-selection rule against the actual profile format. profiles use agent specs parsed by `parse_agent_spec()`, such as `claude`, `codex`, and `hermes:moonshotai/kimi-k2.6`; the plan's ordered auto candidates mix agent names (`claude`, `codex`) with a bare model name (`gpt-5`) and only says to scan execute/plan assignments for appearances. without a precise normalization rule, auto-selection can miss a compared profile using `hermes:openai/gpt-5` and can also hand `run_judge` a bare `claude` or `codex` token that is an agent label in this repo, not the same kind of model identifier as `gpt-5`.",

> DEVELOPER

You are already running inside the megaplan harness for this step. Do the requested planning/review/execution work directly. Do NOT invoke the `megaplan` CLI, do NOT read or activate the `megaplan` skill, do NOT start nested megaplan plans, and do NOT recurse into another planning harness. Treat mentions of megaplan in the repository or environment as implementation context only.

You are an independent reviewer. Critique the plan against the actual repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan





        Idea:
Implement a creative-work mode in megaplan, generalizing today's `--mode joke` into a `--mode creative --form <form>` system with a real provocation engine.

## The provocation engine

The brief fires a small set of provocations at the executor — adversarial prompts that demand an act on the work, not introspection. Three vectors:

- **Cuts** — strip / expose / kill. Example: "Cut the line you'd fight hardest to keep. If the piece is worse, you were right; if it's the same, you killed a darling."
- **Forces** — perform a transformation on the piece. Example: "Halve it. Write the line that survives."
- **Sparks** — the brief itself supplies foreign material the maker reacts to. This is the new vector vs joke mode today. Sub-types:
  - Hostile gifts (an image, line, or detail invented in the brief and asserted to belong)
  - Substitutions ("what if the protagonist were the antagonist of this same scene")
  - Inversions ("the version of this that's a confession, not a performance")
  - Forced transplants (steal a structure from a different form: tarot spread, recipe, courtroom transcript)
  - Borrowed moves (steal one specific move from a master in this form, named)
  - Hostile claims ("this piece is afraid of the thing it's actually about" — refute or absorb)
  - Generative constraints (half the words forbidden, no character speaks, must rhyme)
  - Sensory escalation (what does it smell like / sound like in the next room)
  - Dares (name the move that would get you booed; try it)

Default firing pattern: **one cut, one force, one spark per pass.** Three is plenty; five is a lecture. The prep phase chooses the specific provocations targeted at the draft state — over-explained drafts get cuts on the unsaid; safe drafts get risk/dare sparks; bloated drafts get force-halve; replaceable-feeling drafts get borrowed-move + "who could have made this."

## The stance

Every executor task output includes a stance field: in the maker's authentic voice, naming the provocation engaged, the angle taken, and what changed. Hard constraints to prevent LLM-flavor sand:

- ≤50 words
- First person
- Names the specific provocation engaged
- No hedging verbs (considered / tried / attempted / sought to)
- Must take a position someone could disagree with

A sub-provocation fires at the stance itself: "Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger."

Stance lives in per-task receipts and is bubbled into a "director's notes" sidecar artifact. NOT inline in the final piece — the artifact must stand alone.

## Robustness mapping

Creative work has a different process curve than code: it peaks and then declines into over-sanded mush. Robustness should mean *different angles, harder targets, more diverse provocateur voices* — not just more cycles.

- **Light** — single pass, one cut + one spark (skip the force), stance ≤2 sentences, no critique cycle. For sketches and throwaways. Failure mode protected against: over-process killing a quick instinct.
- **Standard** — one critique/revise cycle. Full triad on prep. Critique fires *different* provocations from a different angle (no relitigating addressed material). Revise stance must explicitly contrast the prior stance.
- **Robust** — multiple cycles with *different provocateur voices*: formalist (structure/economy), iconoclast (risk/lie/dare), audience (stakes/specificity for one named person). Review evaluates full scene canvas + stance authenticity + alignment.

## Executor stop-affordance

Let the maker signal "the next pass would damage the piece" with a stance defending why. Code mode shouldn't have this (more review = safer); creative must. Without it, robust mode reliably over-edits.

## Generalize joke → creative-work

- New mode: `--mode creative --form <form>` with joke as the first registered form
- Existing `--mode joke` continues to work as an alias for `--mode creative --form joke` — no breakage of existing plans
- Prove the abstraction by adding a second form: prep phase picks the cheapest validation form (poem or short ad copy)
- Form-specific prompt fragments inject into shared scaffolds; we do NOT fork five new prompt files per form

## Design branches the prep phase should resolve

The following are real branches with arguments either way — settle them in prep, not in code:

1. Stance field shape: single string vs structured (`challenge_engaged` / `angle_taken` / `what_changed`)
2. Director's notes destination: separate sidecar file, YAML frontmatter on the artifact, or only in receipts
3. Stop-affordance signal: new outcome status, stance flag, or separate halt-task type
4. Provocateur voices in robust mode: fixed roster (formalist / iconoclast / audience) vs model-chosen per cycle
5. Robustness → provocation-count mapping: formal table vs executor discretion within bounds
6. Whether to retire `--primary-criterion` as joke-only and lift it to creative-work-wide, or keep both surfaces

## Out of scope

- New forms beyond the second validation form
- Non-text creative outputs (audio/visual)
- Team-of-makers workflows
- Backwards-compat for any pre-v0.21 joke-mode plan files (current plans untouched; new plans use new schema)

## Primary criterion (for prep guidance)

Sharpest provocation system that survives over-process. The system should produce work that has *a stance*, took risks, made specific choices, and refused something — and should produce that work without grinding it into nervous mush via too many cycles. If the system is "more rigorous" but the output is more sanded, we have failed. The stance authenticity check is the single most load-bearing piece — it is what converts the system from "external pressure" into "training the maker as their own provocateur."

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Creative-Work Mode + Provocation Engine

## Overview

Generalize today's `--mode joke` into `--mode creative --form <form>` with a registered-form system, replace the static joke "lens" critique with a real **provocation engine** (cuts / forces / sparks), add a per-task **stance** field with hard constraints, route stances into a **director's notes sidecar** assembled at the merge boundary, add an executor **stop-affordance** at the correct vertex (the rework branch), and remap robustness so higher levels mean *different angles and provocateur voices*, not more cycles.

**Root-cause framing.** The previous draft fixed the prompt-builder layer but every other mode-aware surface (`mode in {"doc", "joke"}` and `mode == "joke"`) is duplicated across the codebase. Verified instances (from grep):
- `megaplan/evaluation.py:124`, `megaplan/handlers/init.py:64`, `megaplan/handlers/execute.py:106`, `megaplan/handlers/critique.py:120`, `megaplan/cli.py:307`, `megaplan/handlers/review.py:273`
- `megaplan/execute/core.py:163, 169, 209, 308, 401, 468, 1085`
- `megaplan/execute/timeout.py:49, 70, 138, 299`
- `megaplan/schemas.py:653` (`get_execution_schema_key`)
- `megaplan/workers.py:819`, `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:439`
- `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py:128, 137`, `megaplan/prompts/critique.py:302`, `megaplan/prompts/finalize.py:32`, `megaplan/prompts/planning.py:103`, `megaplan/prompts/review.py:85`

These *all* answer one of two questions: "is this a prose-artifact mode?" or "which form's prompt builder do I use?" The fix is two helpers — `is_creative_mode(state) -> bool` and `creative_form_id(state) -> str | None` — added once, called everywhere. Same shape as the existing `configured_robustness(state)` helper. This is a mechanical sweep, not new logic.

**Workflow override (FLAG-001 / issue_hints-1).** `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES['standard']` at `megaplan/_core/workflow.py:90-94` maps `STATE_INITIALIZED → plan` (skipping prep). The brief says standard creative mode fires the full triad on prep. Two ways to honor this: (a) branch the override on mode so creative-standard runs prep, or (b) move provocation *selection* out of prep so it runs at critique time too. We pick (b) for code mode (no change) and (a) for creative mode: provocation selection lives in a pure function callable from both prep and critique, and the workflow override is mode-aware so creative-standard runs prep (where the *initial* triad is selected and recorded), then critique re-selects against the prior triad. This is one workflow-override change + one selection-helper that doesn't care which step calls it.

**Stop-affordance at the correct vertex (FLAG-002 / issue_hints-2).** `_resolve_review_outcome()` at `megaplan/handlers/review.py:149-201` already returns `STATE_DONE` for `approved` reviews. The damaging extra pass happens on the `needs_rework` branch (line 191). The check belongs on the rework branch: when `mode is creative` AND any `task_updates[].stop_signal.requested == true` AND `review_verdict == "needs_rework"`, treat it as a maker-honored halt — return `success`, `STATE_DONE` with the defense logged to `meta.notes` and `directors_notes.json`. Robust-mode pre-critique cycles also need a check, see Step 9.

**Director's notes at merge boundary (scope-2).** `_merge_batch_results()` (`megaplan/execute/core.py:307-315`) and `_build_aggregate_execution_payload()` already normalize task updates into `finalize.json`. The director's-notes assembler hooks into those merge points, not a separate artifact crawler.

**Joke alias as a thin shim.** `--mode joke` continues to work. At init time it's stored as `mode = "joke"` (preserved verbatim for in-flight plans) AND `form = "joke"` is stamped onto config. The two helpers (`is_creative_mode`, `creative_form_id`) treat `mode in {"creative", "joke"}` as creative and resolve form from `state.config.form` (or `"joke"` when missing).

The whole change is contained inside `megaplan/cli.py`, `megaplan/handlers/`, `megaplan/prompts/`, `megaplan/audits/robustness.py`, `megaplan/schemas.py`, `megaplan/_core/`, `megaplan/execute/core.py`, `megaplan/execute/timeout.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`, `megaplan/hermes_worker.py`, `megaplan/evaluation.py`, plus a new `megaplan/forms/` package and tests under `tests/`.

## Phase 1: Foundation — mode helpers, forms registry, schemas, workflow override

### Step 1: Add mode helpers (`megaplan/_core/modes.py`, re-export from `megaplan/_core/__init__.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `megaplan/_core/modes.py`:
   ```python
   def is_creative_mode(state: PlanState) -> bool:
       mode = state.get("config", {}).get("mode", "code")
       return mode in {"creative", "joke"}

   def creative_form_id(state: PlanState) -> str | None:
       if not is_creative_mode(state):
           return None
       form = state.get("config", {}).get("form")
       if isinstance(form, str) and form.strip():
           return form.strip()
       # Back-compat: legacy joke plans have mode="joke", no form key.
       if state.get("config", {}).get("mode") == "joke":
           return "joke"
       return None

   def is_prose_mode(state: PlanState) -> bool:
       """True for any mode that writes a prose artifact (doc, metaplan-as-doc, creative)."""
       mode = state.get("config", {}).get("mode", "code")
       return mode in {"doc", "joke", "creative"}
   ```
2. **Re-export** from `megaplan/_core/__init__.py` so existing call sites can `from megaplan._core import is_creative_mode, creative_form_id, is_prose_mode`.
3. **Note for Step 6 sweep:** every `mode in {"doc", "joke"}` is logically `is_prose_mode(state)`. Every `mode == "joke"` is logically `is_creative_mode(state)` (joke today, joke+poem after this lands).

**Scope:** Medium

   - `ProvocateurVoice` dataclass: `id`, `persona_text`, `vector_bias: tuple[str, ...]`.
   - `FORMS: dict[str, Form]`, `register(form)`, `get_form(id)`, `available_form_ids() -> tuple[str, ...]`.

2. **Add** `megaplan/forms/joke.py`: migrate the 9 existing joke lenses to `Provocation(vector="spark", subtype=…)` (e.g. `subtext_inversion → spark/inversion`, `genre_swap → spark/forced_transplant`). Add 3 cuts ("cut the line you'd fight hardest to keep", "kill the explanation", "strip the laugh-track beat") and 3 forces ("halve it", "no character speaks", "must rhyme by the button"). Beat IDs: `("opening","inciting","obstacle","turn","button")`. Voices: `formalist`, `iconoclast`, `audience`.

3. **Add** `megaplan/forms/poem.py`: beat IDs `("opening_image","turn","close")`. ≥3 each of cuts/forces/sparks (e.g. cut "kill the abstraction", force "halve it. write the line that survives.", spark "borrow one move from a master poet, named — and name them"). Voices: same fixed roster.

4. **Register** both forms at module import time. Add `FORMS["joke"]` and `FORMS["poem"]`.

## Phase 3: Provocation engine + stance + stop-affordance

### Step 8: Provocation selection (`megaplan/forms/provocations.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Add** `select_provocations(form, *, robustness, iteration, draft_state_tags, prior_provocation_ids) -> tuple[Provocation, ...]`. **Resolves branch #5 (formal table within bounds):**
   - `light`: `(1 cut, 1 spark)`.
   - `standard`: `(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark)`. On critique iteration N>1, exclude `prior_provocation_ids`.
   - `robust` / `superrobust`: `(1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark)` per cycle, biased by the cycle's voice's `vector_bias`.
2. **Add** `select_provocateur_voice(form, iteration) -> ProvocateurVoice | None`: rotates `formalist → iconoclast → audience` for `robust`/`superrobust`; returns `None` for light/standard. **Resolves branch #4.**
3. **Add** `_draft_state_tags(state, plan_dir) -> tuple[str, ...]`: heuristics on the latest plan markdown. Tag `over_explained`, `safe`, `bloated`, `replaceable`. Pure, deterministic.
4. **Add** `select_active_checks(state, robustness) -> tuple[CritiqueCheckSpec, ...]`: the dispatcher that wraps `select_provocations` for the active form (or falls back to `checks_for_robustness(robustness)` when `not is_creative_mode(state)`). Reads prior provocation IDs from `directors_notes.json` if it exists. This single function is what `handle_critique`, the Hermes template rewriter, and the worker mocks all call.

### Step 13: Stance validation enforcement (`megaplan/execute/core.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Update** the merge step (`megaplan/execute/core.py:_merge_batch_results`) to call `validate_stance(task_update.stance)` for each task update with a stance. Violations are stored on the task as `stance_violations: list[str]` (added to `directors_notes.json`'s `stances[]` entries).
2. **Bubble** violations into the next critique pass: the critique prompt builder (Step 9) reads `directors_notes.json` and, if the prior pass had `stance_violations`, prepends a stance-authenticity sub-provocation per the brief: "Read it aloud. If it's safe, the work is too. Rewrite the part that would embarrass you to say to a stranger."
3. **Soft enforcement only** (no hard reject — preserves the brief's "don't grind into nervous mush" principle).
4. **Resolves "Stance authenticity check fires as sub-provocation."**

---

## Phase 4: Tests + validation

### Step 14: Pure-function unit tests (`tests/test_modes_helpers.py`, `tests/test_forms_registry.py`, `tests/test_provocations.py`, `tests/test_stance_validator.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **`tests/test_modes_helpers.py`**: `is_creative_mode` true for `mode=creative` and `mode=joke`; `creative_form_id` returns `"joke"` for legacy joke state with no form key, returns `state.config.form` for new state, returns `None` for code mode. `is_prose_mode` true for doc/joke/creative.
2. **`tests/test_forms_registry.py`**: `get_form("joke")` and `get_form("poem")` exist; both have ≥3 cuts/forces/sparks; joke has 5 beat IDs, poem has 3; both declare `execution_schema_key == "execution_doc.json"`.
3. **`tests/test_provocations.py`**: light=2, standard=3, robust=3 with rotating voice; `prior_provocation_ids` filters duplicates on standard iteration 2; `over_explained` tag biases selection.
4. **`tests/test_stance_validator.py`**: valid passes; >50 words → violation; hedging verb → violation; third-person → violation; no claim marker → violation.

### Step 15: Workflow + handler integration tests (`tests/test_handle_init_creative_mode.py`, `tests/test_workflow_creative.py`, `tests/test_review_stop_affordance.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **`tests/test_handle_init_creative_mode.py`**: `--mode creative --form joke --output …` succeeds and writes `state.config.form="joke"`; `--mode creative` without `--form` raises `invalid_args`; `--mode creative --form poem --primary-criterion "tightest image"` accepted.
2. **Update** `tests/test_handle_init_joke_mode.py`: `--mode joke` still works AND now writes `state.config.form="joke"`.
3. **`tests/test_workflow_creative.py`**: build a synthetic creative-mode state at `STATE_INITIALIZED` with `robustness=standard`; assert `workflow_next()` returns `prep` (not `plan`). Same for joke alias. Code-mode standard still returns `plan`.
4. **`tests/test_review_stop_affordance.py`**: build a state with a needs_rework review and a `stop_signal.requested=true` task in finalize; assert `_resolve_review_outcome()` returns `("success", STATE_DONE, None)` and `meta.notes` records the defense. Without the stop signal, the same input returns `("needs_rework", STATE_FINALIZED, "execute")` (existing behavior preserved). Code mode with stop_signal does NOT short-circuit (helper returns False).

### Step 16: Schema + runtime-layout tests (`tests/test_runtime_layout_creative.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `tests/test_runtime_layout_creative.py`: call `ensure_runtime_layout()`; assert `.megaplan/schemas/execution_doc.json` exists and includes `stance` + `stop_signal` properties; assert `.megaplan/schemas/directors_notes.json` exists; assert `get_execution_schema_key("creative", "poem") == "execution_doc.json"`.

### Step 17: Prompt builder tests (`tests/test_prompts_creative.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **`tests/test_prompts_creative.py`**:
   - `_critique_creative_prompt(form=joke, robustness="standard")` mentions exactly 1 cut, 1 force, 1 spark by name.
   - `_critique_creative_prompt(form=poem, ...)` references poem beats, not screenplay beats.
   - `_execute_creative_prompt` includes stance + stop-affordance instructions.
   - Code-mode `_execute_prompt` does NOT include stop-affordance instructions.
   - Iteration 2 critique excludes provocation IDs from a fixture `directors_notes.json`.
   - Dispatcher: `create_claude_prompt("critique", state_with_mode_joke, …)` ≡ `create_claude_prompt("critique", state_with_mode_creative_form_joke, …)`.
   - `select_active_checks(state, robustness)` returns the same output as `joke_checks_for_robustness(robustness)` for joke (back-compat).

### Step 18: End-to-end smoke (`tests/test_creative_mode_smoke.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** smoke test with mocked agents:
   - `--mode creative --form poem --output poems/p.md` → prep+plan+critique+revise+execute+review.
   - Mocked execute returns a valid stance + `stop_signal.requested=false`. Assert `directors_notes.json` is written via the merge boundary, contains form, primary_criterion, fired provocations, stances.
   - Second run: mocked execute returns `stop_signal.requested=true`, mocked review returns `needs_rework`. Assert review halts with STATE_DONE and `meta.notes` records the defense.

### Step 19: Run focused tests, then full suite
**Scope:** Small

1. **Run** unit tests (Steps 14, 16) — fastest, no fixtures.
2. **Run** init/workflow/review tests (Step 15).
3. **Run** prompt builder tests (Step 17).
4. **Run** smoke (Step 18) and existing `tests/test_joke_mode_smoke.py`.
5. **Run** full `pytest tests/` to catch regressions, especially anywhere a `mode in {"doc", "joke"}` site was rewritten.

---

## Execution Order

1. **Phase 1** (helpers + registry + schemas + workflow) lands first. Every later phase depends on the helpers existing.
2. Inside Phase 1: Step 1 (helpers) → Step 2 (forms registry) → Step 3 (init/CLI) → Step 4 (workflow override) → Step 5 (schemas + runtime layout). Steps 1 and 2 can land in either order; Step 3 depends on both.
3. **Phase 2** (mode-aware sweep) is mechanical and runs after Phase 1. Each replaced site gets its file-local pytest before moving on.
4. **Phase 3** (provocation engine + stance + stop-affordance + sidecar) depends on Steps 1, 2, 6 being done. Step 8 (selection) → Step 9 (critique/revise prompts) → Step 10 (stance + execute prompt) → Step 11 (stop-affordance handler) → Step 12 (sidecar at merge) → Step 13 (stance validation enforcement).
5. **Phase 4 tests** can begin in parallel with Phase 3 once Steps 1-8 are committed (all pure functions are testable on their own).
6. The riskiest moment is Step 6 (mode sweep); land it green before starting Phase 3.

## Validation Order

1. Pure-function unit tests (helpers, registry, selection, stance validator) — no fixtures.
2. Schema + runtime-layout tests — light I/O only.
3. Workflow + handler tests — fixture-based but no agent calls.
4. Prompt builder tests — fixture-based, deterministic.
5. End-to-end smoke tests with mocked agents.
6. Full pytest suite to catch regressions in adjacent code (especially `tests/bakeoff/*` and any file touched by the Phase 2 sweep).

    "Legacy joke state (mode='joke', no form key) is handled by `creative_form_id()`'s fallback to 'joke', not by a one-time migration. The brief explicitly lifts the back-compat requirement for pre-v0.21 plan files, so this is convenience, not contract.",
    "Forms declare their own `execution_schema_key` (defaulting to `execution_doc.json`). Both joke and poem use `execution_doc.json` since `sections_written` works for any prose-with-beats form.",
    "The `--mode joke` value remains stored verbatim in state (not normalized to 'creative') so that any legacy code or external tooling string-equaling on it still works during the transition. The helpers treat both as creative.",
    "`select_provocations` reads prior provocation IDs from `directors_notes.json`; on iteration 1 (no prior file), the prior set is empty and selection is unconstrained.",
    "The mode-aware `_CREATIVE_OVERRIDES` for the workflow lives in `megaplan/_core/workflow.py` next to `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES`. Layering it via `workflow_next()` is less invasive than making `_ROBUSTNESS_OVERRIDES` mode-aware and lets code-mode behavior stay untouched.",
    "Stance validator stays soft-enforced: violations log + bubble into the next critique as a stance-authenticity sub-provocation rather than hard-reject. Hard reject would force re-runs for hedging-verb violations and contradict the brief's 'don't grind into nervous mush' principle.",
    "directors_notes.json is JSON only; markdown rendering is a follow-up if requested.",
    "Robust mode rotates a fixed roster (formalist/iconoclast/audience). Model-chosen voices can be a config opt-in later if needed.",
    "`--primary-criterion` is lifted to all `--mode creative` (any form), per the brief's framing of primary criterion as load-bearing for creative work.",
    "Poem (not ad copy) is the second validation form because it stresses the abstraction harder via different beat IDs and a different stance voice.",
    "Step 13's stance validation runs at merge time (so `directors_notes.json`'s `stance_violations` field is populated by the merge boundary, consistent with scope-2). The next critique reads it from the sidecar."

    "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Execution schema and merge semantics: the plan adds `mode='creative'`, but the current code treats prose artifacts by checking `mode in {'doc', 'joke'}` in `get_execution_schema_key()` (`megaplan/schemas.py:652-653`), `_merge_batch_results()` (`megaplan/execute/core.py:307-315`), execute evidence checks (`megaplan/execute/core.py:1087-1104`), and timeout recovery (`megaplan/execute/timeout.py:54-67`, `139-145`). Unless the plan explicitly updates these mode checks to include creative prose modes, a `--mode creative --form poem` execution will be validated and merged like code, requiring `files_changed`/`commands_run` instead of `sections_written`.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "correctness-2",
    "concern": "Are the proposed changes technically correct?: Prompt dispatch correctness: the plan covers `megaplan/prompts/__init__.py`, but the active critique worker selection also computes expected check IDs in `megaplan/handlers/critique.py:107-124`, mock critique payloads in `megaplan/workers.py:810-818`, and Hermes output-template rewriting in `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:430-438` using `mode == 'joke'` rather than a form resolver. Those paths must be updated or creative+joke and creative+poem critique outputs can be validated against the wrong checks or rendered with the wrong output template.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "scope-1",
    "concern": "Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: Mode aliasing is broader than init and prompt builders: `rg` found mode-specific surfaces in CLI status display (`megaplan/cli.py:304-307`), execute timeout auditing (`megaplan/execute/timeout.py:77-84`), review pre-check gating (`megaplan/handlers/review.py:283-286`), schema selection, execute merge, workers, and Hermes. The plan's file list mentions some of these categories generically, but it does not call out that every prose-artifact check currently written as `doc|joke` needs a creative/form-aware helper.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "scope-2",
    "concern": "Search for related code that handles the same concept. Is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: The plan's proposed directors-notes assembler walks plan-dir artifacts for per-iteration critique flags, finalize stances, and execute stop signals, but current execution merge already normalizes task updates into `finalize.json` in `_merge_batch_results()` and aggregate payloads in `_build_aggregate_execution_payload()`. This broader data-flow suggests the sidecar writer should be integrated at merge/review boundaries rather than implemented as a separate artifact crawler that can drift from the source-of-truth merge logic.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "all_locations-1",
    "concern": "Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?: Generated/runtime schema infrastructure is a required location. `ensure_runtime_layout()` writes every schema from `SCHEMAS` to `.megaplan/schemas` (`megaplan/_core/io.py:306-312`), and worker invocations read those disk schemas via `schemas_root()` (`megaplan/workers.py:1289-1290`, `1395-1396`; `megaplan/hermes_worker.py:281-283`). The plan updates `SCHEMAS` but does not include a runtime-schema assertion for `execution_doc.json`/creative mode or any new sidecar schema, so schema additions may not be tested through the same path agents actually consume.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "all_locations-2",
    "concern": "Does the change touch all locations AND supporting infrastructure?: The plan says to keep `--mode joke` working as an alias while new plans set `mode='creative'` and `form='joke'`, but it does not include a migration/helper contract for existing state files where `mode='joke'` and no `form` exists outside prompt dispatch. Current callers read `state['config'].get('mode')` directly across execute, review, workers, and CLI status; without a shared `is_creative_mode()`/`creative_form_id()` helper, it is easy for back-compat to work in prompts but fail in execution or validation.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "callers-1",
    "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?: Callers of execution validation pass only the plan mode string, not the form. `workers.py`, `hermes_worker.py`, and execute merge code call `get_execution_schema_key(plan_mode)` or branch on `mode` only, so the planned `--mode creative --form poem` path will not have access to form-specific evidence requirements unless the plan changes those callers or makes all creative forms share the document execution schema.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  },
  {
    "id": "callers-2",
    "concern": "Find the callers of the changed function. What arguments do they actually pass? Does the fix handle all of them?: Callers of critique check selection pass only robustness today. `handle_critique()`, `run_parallel_critique()`, default mocks, and Hermes template rewriting all use `checks_for_robustness(robustness)` or `joke_checks_for_robustness(robustness)`, so the planned `form_provocations_as_checks(form, robustness)` needs caller changes that pass the resolved form, not just a back-compatible rewrite of `joke_checks_for_robustness()`.",
    "status": "addressed",
    "severity": "significant"
  }
]

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the real callers for these paths are shell commands that expect actual files. reigh's readme uploads an idea file into `/workspace/megaplan-idea-2week.txt` before calling `megaplan init`, and `chain.sh` immediately checks `-f \"$spec\"` before running `megaplan chain --spec \"$spec\"`. the revised plan now constructs equivalent callers from `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec`, but still never adds the corresponding file-transfer or materialization step, so those callers would receive nonexistent arguments.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-megaplan-cloud-a-new-20260421-0406"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-065",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: batch execute callers still need a clearer identity contract. `handle_execute_one_batch()` can be invoked once per global batch in the same plan iteration, and the revised plan tells every invocation to use `phase=\"execute\"`, `iteration=state[\"iteration\"]`, and `attempt=1`. those actual caller arguments are not enough to distinguish batch receipts unless the plan changes the filename/attempt semantics or emits only one final aggregate receipt for `--batch` runs.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint1-step-receipts"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-070",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the planned judge resolver's inputs against `load_profiles()` and `parse_agent_spec()`. the caller will receive profile phase values as strings like `claude`, `codex`, or `hermes:glm-5.1`; to handle all actual values, the resolver should parse each spec into `(agent, model)` and compare canonical agent/model identities rather than doing raw string membership against `['claude', 'codex', 'gpt-5']`.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint2-bakeoff"
      ]
    }

      "id": "DEBT-049",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: input provisioning is the broader unresolved concept here. the repo's current railway workflow does not just run commands; it also stages runtime artifacts into `/workspace` first, such as the uploaded idea text in readme line 103 and the expected `/workspace/chain.yaml` consumed by `chain.sh`. the revised plan fixes the runner dispatch itself, but it still treats `auto.idea_file` and `chain.spec` as if those files will already exist without describing how cloud deploy makes that true.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
